# klack — progress

Canonical status. The plan and architecture are in `DESIGN.md`. The reverse-engineered
protocol notes are in `docs/protocol/`; dev and test helpers in `tools/`. (Until H1 this repository
lived inside a larger reverse-engineering workspace; references to it were removed then. Until H2
the project was called slacktui: rows before H2 use that name, its `SLACKTUI_*` variables and its
folders, as they were then.)

**Verification levels**
- **LIVE**: exercised in a real kitty window against a test workspace and checked by
  screenshot and/or by reading back as the other account.
- **UNIT**: covered by `cargo test` only.
- **NONE**: written but not exercised.

| C3 — clicking a desktop notification opens its conversation: on XDG desktops (`cfg(all(unix, not(target_os = "macos")))`) the notification declares the "default" action and is shown with notify-rust's async API; a task waits (zbus futures, no blocked thread) up to 30 minutes for the click — swaync keeps notifications in its centre — and sends `Net::NotificationClicked(Target)` to the event loop, which opens it like a search hit (message selected; a thread reply opens the thread with the reply selected) or, for a huddle that started, the conversation with the composer focused. | F3c — stray picture fragments beside popups: not a cut wide character, as guessed, but kitty's Unicode placeholders. Each cell of a picture draws its own slice, and only the first cell of each row says which row/column it is; the cells after it are numbered from their left neighbour. A popup over part of a picture therefore left half an avatar beside its left border, and the cells right of it lost their numbering and showed a wrong slice (the "vk"). | F3e — the old room right after a huddle ended: reproduced once in ~15 runs of B alone in #social (sessions of 5–10 s, the next one started 0–25 s later): `rooms.join` returned the previous room **and the same attendee id**, and the session ended before connecting, with no error (a clean close: the meeting was over); every other time the room was either still live (reused fine) or a new one. Not a slacktui bug but a race on Slack's side, so the TUI now copes: a call whose session ends before it connected — and not because we left — is joined again once after 2 s ("the huddle ended before connecting; joining again" in the log); if that ends the same way: "cannot join the huddle: it ended as we joined". `Call` gained `leaving` (set by `leave`) so a leave while connecting never rejoins | done | LIVE (the race itself: seen with `ctl huddle-media`, not again after the fix, so the rejoin path is not seen live; regression: F3 then F3 while the bar said "connecting…" → "leaving the huddle…" → "left the huddle", no rejoin in the log; an ordinary join ("🎧 #social · just you") and leave still work; temporary settings.toml with the huddle sounds off, removed) + UNIT (the decision: ended before connecting → join again, a second time → give up, connected or leaving → an ordinary end) | c897e5f |
| F3f — the cost of a long history: measured with a synthetic timeline (`build_lines_cost`, an ignored test): building the message pane's lines — done on every frame — took 2.2 / 11.6 / 52.5 ms for 1 000 / 5 000 / 20 000 messages in a release build (about 4× that in debug). The pane now builds only what it needs: `build_lines_from` starts at any shown message, seeded with what the whole build would carry over (the previous author for grouping — none after a system message — and the previous day for separators); a draw builds the newest 100 messages, more (doubling) until the scroll position plus a screen is covered, and always the selected message and the one being edited; a thread is built whole. While only part is built, "at the top" (which loads older history) and the "beginning of conversation" note do not apply. At the bottom a frame now costs 0.23 / 0.24 / 0.28 ms for the same sizes. | T3b — fewer malloc arenas: glibc gives every allocating thread its own arena and keeps freed memory in it; with tokio's worker per core (32 here, 34 threads) that held megabytes. `main` now sets `mallopt(M_ARENA_MAX, 2)` (Linux / glibc only) before building the runtime itself (no more `#[tokio::main]`). | U2 — keymap engine: two leaders, the pane before global, the user wins. `leader` (default `space`) and `insert_leader` (default `ctrl+space`, `""` = none) in keys.toml; `<leader>` in a sequence stands for either: a pressed key reads as the leader when it is the insert leader, or the leader in NORMAL (or a leader that never types), and falls back to its plain key when it starts nothing (Space still folds a sidebar section while no leader sequence is bound). `Keymap::resolve(contexts, mode, pending, key)` replaces `lookup`: contexts most specific first (the dialog, or the suggestion list and the pane, then global); the first context that knows the sequence decides; in INSERT and dialogs a `[global]` sequence starting with a plain character is left out (it types), in a dialog only `[global]` leader sequences and quit count — so a printable `[global]` key may be bound now and acts in NORMAL only. `Pending` keeps the sequence (as looked up and as pressed) and the mode it began in; a mode change drops it (before every key and every draw). `Keymap::show` writes sequences short for the status bar (`␣h`, `^␣h`, `^B`, `M-x`). In keys.toml the user's sequences are bound first: a default that equals, starts or extends one gives way with a warning naming it; two of the user's that collide: the first in the table keeps it (also for prefixes now). | V7 fix — "until" for quiet periods longer than a day (review): a dnd.info window over 24 h went through the daily rule (`clock::window_start`), so a custom day paused all day (Tue 00:00 → Wed 08:00) or a paused weekend read as notifications on. dnd.info alone can't tell such a period from Slack's daily report (00:00 today → 09:00 tomorrow, seen 15:20): both start at midnight in the past and run ~32 h. | V18 — a thread opened from the huddle view (user: closing it with Esc closed the huddle view too). Cause: `close_thread` always moved the focus to Messages, and `draw` closes the huddle view whenever Messages has the focus. Now `focus_after_thread` sends the focus back to the huddle view when it is shown; in a narrow window (the thread takes the view's place) the view is parked in `parked_huddle` and comes back when the thread closes. Also from the V14 review: the restored thread (`Net::ThreadBack`) moves the focus to itself only if it is still on the messages (`focus_after_thread_back`), and `app.start` is cleared when another conversation opens | done | UNIT: `closing_a_thread_returns_to_the_huddle_view`. | V19 — a screen share fills its tile (user: "screen sharing doesnt occupy the entire tile in huddle view. thus maximizing the tile does absolutely nothing"). Cause: `draw_video` encoded frames with ratatui-image's `Resize::Fit`, which only shrinks: a frame smaller than the tile (the 640×360 synthetic share, any low-resolution stream) stayed at its own pixel size, so a bigger or maximized tile drew the same small picture. Now `video_cells` (frame px + tile cells + the picker's cell px → cells, aspect kept, never past the tile, at least one cell) sizes the picture and `Resize::Scale` draws it at that size, centred; camera tiles get the same | done | UNIT: `video_fills_its_tile` (small tile, maximized, big frame into a small tile, tall frame, very wide frame, 1×1, empty tile/frame, unknown font size), `a_small_frame_is_drawn_tile_sized` (RED first: a 64×36 frame was drawn 7×2 cells in an 80×30 tile). LIVE on test account A (muted huddle in the A-B DM with B's `--share-test`, sounds off, settings.toml restored): `␣hv` showed the 640×360 share filling the stage width (~705×396 px), Enter maximized it to the tile height (~864×486 px), Esc restored; 🔇 muted throughout; left at once. Fix round 1 (review): upscaled with `FilterType::Triangle` instead of Nearest (`video_resize`, smoother text on a shared screen); UNIT `video_is_scaled_smoothly` (RED first), the draw test asserts the half-block picker's 10×20 cells; NOT live (filter only) | 0a4f8ed, 0a4f8ed, 75505e5 |

| V21 — the notification schedule no longer flickers back (user: "when switching from one option to another (e.g. every day -> weekdays -> custom -> off) the settings toggles back to the previous value for a brief moment"). Cause (seen live with `ctl listen` on A): Slack echoes every schedule write as ~26 `pref_change` events, one per `dnd_*` pref, `dnd_days` last; each one rebuilt the schedule from klack's copy of Slack's prefs, which still had the old kind, over the change klack showed at once — until the last event. Fix: `ScheduleQueue` keeps the edits in flight (`flying`, `pending()`), and `show_pending_schedule` lays them (and those waiting) over Slack's copy after every `dnd_*` `pref_change` and after each read-back | done | UNIT: `slacks_echo_of_a_write_never_shows_the_previous_kind` (RED first against a stub: "after dnd_start_hour: the latest change stays shown"). LIVE on test account A (F6 → Notification schedule, Enter ×4, the row sampled by `get-text` for 3.5 s after each): without the fix `weekdays → every day → weekdays`, `custom → weekdays → custom`, `off → custom → off`, `every day → off → every day`; with it `weekdays`, `custom`, `off`, `every day`, and three quick Enters only `every day`. A's `dnd_*` prefs restored exactly (diff clean); settings.toml and drafts unchanged | ad7ee31 |

| VX5 — why test huddles ended after ~10 min, and why invitations didn't reach A | done | FINDINGS (docs/protocol/huddles.md): (1) the 10-min end was klack's: about 10 min into a call Chime's TURN server can answer the channel-binding refresh with 438 Stale Nonce; webrtc-rs' TURN client (rtc-turn 0.21.1) then drops the binding instead of retrying, incoming media stops, the connection fails and klack left — with both sides klack the huddle emptied and ended. Not silence, not the free plan, not Chime's alone end (AUDIO_STATUS 410, never sent), no missing keep-alive. Fix: a lost media connection (failed, or disconnected 4 s) joins again — new signaling, JOIN, peer connection, SUBSCRIBE with the same token, as the Chime SDK's reconnect — keeping mute/camera, up to 3 failed tries (0/2/4 s); status line "huddle connection lost — joining again…" / "back in the huddle"; AUDIO_STATUS logged; debug-only `KLACK_HUDDLE_DROP=SECS` test hook. (2) invitations: no change — Slack withholds `huddle_invite` from an invitee in Do Not Disturb (VX2 ran inside the 17:00–09:00 schedule); shape unchanged. UNIT: `a_lost_connection_is_one_that_failed_or_stays_disconnected`, `reconnects_are_bounded_and_back_off` / LIVE (test workspace, fake home, sounds off, nonexistent mic/speaker, joined muted, no klack source-outputs): run 1 (old build) both media dropped at 10:00–10:02 → huddle ended; diagnostic run with TURN logs lasted 11:45 (no 438 that time); forced drop at 40 s → B connected again 3 s later, one `sh_room_join`, huddle went on; 13:20 run on the fix: B's ChannelBind got 438 at 10:00, B re-joined and was back ~12 s later, A unaffected, huddle lasted to the end. Invites: A out of DND → `huddle_invite` + ring, B left after 6 s → `huddle_invite_cancel` stopped the ring at +15 s (< 30 s timeout); A snoozed (`dnd.setSnooze`, ended after; dnd.info + dnd_* prefs of A and B equal the snapshot) → no invite, no cancel. Every huddle ended (0 live) / NOT: the ~12 s gap at the 438 (needs an rtc-turn fix: retry ChannelBind and allocation refresh on 438), a reconnect while unmuted or with the camera on, a lost signaling socket (still ends the call) | a9a11d3 |

| VY4 — back and forward (item 8), as a browser does: Ctrl+O / Alt+← back, Alt+→ forward (`[global] go_back`, `go_forward`; F1, F6 → Keys, README). A place is a conversation (with the thread open in it, if any), the Threads view, the Activity view on its tab, or the Huddles view; the last 20 back, per workspace (kept in memory across workspace switches, for the run). Recorded where every way of going meets: after each event the loop compares where the main pane is with where it was — a key, a click or a notification click (and a reply that goes where you asked: a link's conversation looked up, a DM opened, a channel joined or created) keeps the place left and drops what was ahead; any other reply (a page that opens the thread a link led to, a conversation gone) only stands in for the place, so a step is never split in two. Going back reopens the place as opening it does (jump to / open the hit with its thread / the view on its tab), and where it lands is not a new step. A channel left (you were in it then) or archived, or a conversation unknown now, is skipped and forgotten; nowhere to go: "nowhere to go back to" / "… forward to" in the status line (through `tr`). Only in NORMAL: the actions do not count while typing or in a dialog (`keymap::counts`). The Activity view's tab changes in place (not a step). The spot is not part of the place's identity, so a selection or a scroll never makes a step; the entry is updated after every event (as before) and, for an event that selects and leaves at once (a click on an Activity or feed item), at the first point the pane is about to change (`History::note`, from `set_view`, `sync_current`, `open_thread_at`, `close_thread`, `open_hit_as`). The views restore their item once their list is in (`Threads::want`, `Activity::want`; not in it: the first, as before). A message re-selected is brought into view again (`MessagePane::select`). ## Next (in order, each as a small, separately verified step)

**v0.3.0 released** 2026-10-19 (https://github.com/dmytrobsp/klack/releases/tag/v0.3.0; asked by the user before going afk; CI green on the bump 3e8caca, four builds, the Linux download verified against SHA256SUMS and run: `klack 0.3.0`). It carries queues J (easier sign-in), K (sidebar like Slack's) and L (fixes from real use). **v0.4.0 released** 2026-10-19 (https://github.com/dmytrobsp/klack/releases/tag/v0.4.0; asked by the user: "yes, publish"; CI green on the bump 9ff655d; the x86_64 macOS build first failed on the runner ("ranlib: can't write to output file (Input/output error)" while building SQLite) and passed when that job was run again; four builds, the Linux download verified against SHA256SUMS and run: `klack 0.4.0`). M. Asked by the user on 2026-10-19 (after v0.3.0); one verified commit per step:

      before; arboard 3.7 reads all three on Linux (Wayland and X11), macOS and Windows.
  ~~M3~~ — done. Attachments in the composer: Ctrl+V (rebindable) in the composer or the reply box: an image becomes
      `image.png`, copied files keep their names, text is inserted at the cursor; nothing usable (or no clipboard,
      e.g. over SSH) → a note in the status bar. One line per attachment above the box (`📎 image.png · 214 KB ✕`);
      ✕ (click) removes one, Backspace in an empty box the last one; the text draft is untouched.
  ~~M4~~ — done. Sending: Enter sends all attachments plus the text (or no text at all: the user wants to send just an image)
      as ONE post, with the usual upload progress; into the thread from the reply box; on failure the attachments
      and text stay so it can be retried.
  ~~M5~~ — done. Threads, README/help, live tests with `wl-copy` (the user allowed overwriting their clipboard).
  The user's calls: Ctrl+V; images and file-manager files (asked "maybe more than images?" → yes, files too);
  an image alone can be sent. Claude's calls: attachments are not kept in drafts (lost on quit, the text stays);

(5) **Follow-ups from queue VW** (deferred by the reviews, none blocking): live status pushes never reach klack's rtm.connect connection (the web client's own gateway with `flannel=3` might — a probe was refused by the safety classifier because it puts the token in a URL; today a users.info refresh every 60 s); a batched Flannel `users/info` could replace the per-user refresh (~50 calls/min); klack sends no `rooms.leave` when leaving a huddle (the official client does; Slack clears the state anyway); test huddles end after ~10 min (cause unknown — free plan?); the Activity count in the sidebar updates only at start/when the view loads; Activity rebuilds per frame and `mpim_people` scans all users (large workspaces); a huddle_start refetch may swallow a 'started a huddle' note; 307/308 redirects re-send a body (custom redirect policy); draft tests write a fake T0-U0 draft into the real data folder (use a temp XDG_DATA_HOME); small: README `q` alias, '1 h 0 min', a raw channel id for unknown channels in Recent.

W. Asked by the user on 2026-10-20 (after V20), "more features we need to add" — brainstorm first (superpowers:brainstorming), draft
   docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-20-prefs-help-and-auth-design.md:
   1. Contextual help in F6: "right now every section in preferences just prints a bunch of text in the lower right part. it's hard to
      read because a) its not formatted and b) it describes every single thing that's in the section. instead every item inside the
      section should display its own text. and it should be neatly formatted so its easy to read".
   2. Interactive login + `auth` commands: when klack starts under a profile that isn't signed in to a workspace, or with `login`, it
      guides the user — ask for the workspace name (add `.slack.com` if missing), accept a URL pasted from a browser, offer the login
      methods and let the user pick one. "the entire `login` argument should instead be replaced with `auth` family of commands. that
      should allow for login/logout/status so user can control the workspaces he's signed into. these still should mesh with
      `--profile`, so that user can still have separate profiles with different workspaces."

V. (V2–V27 done 2026-10-20: clicks in jump/search/threads/activity/F6/details, hover in dialogs, the reaction picker lists yours first
   and who reacted, DND pause + schedule, presence dot, start in NORMAL + thread restore, huddle view polish, screen share fills its tile,
   search shows files, mute / unmute (`␣cM`), mark unread (`␣cu`), next / previous unread (Alt+Shift+↑/↓), copy a conversation's link
   (`␣cy`). The huddle invitation was re-tested (V27): it rings outside Do Not Disturb — V1 found Slack withholds invites during DND;
   `/dnd off` now ends a scheduled quiet period. Still open: only the leftovers and wishes below.)
   Bugs and wishes the user found in the
   v0.5.0 UI (2026-10-19): clicks in the Threads view went through to the messages (U1 stops the fall-through;
   clicking a row should pick / open it — Threads, Activity, Ctrl+K, search); the reaction picker should list your
   own reactions on the message first, clearly marked so they are easy to remove, then other people's, then the
   rest (done; V26 shows who reacted). (The menu not running items on their shown key is fixed in U7.)
   Small leftovers from U's reviews (none blocks; pick up when touching the area): paste into dialog text fields
   (Ctrl+K, search, picker, F6 fields) is dropped; the wheel does nothing over Details / profile / snippet / viewer;
   which-key offers huddle actions with no call; the which-key page isn't reset when the prefix changes and the last
   page's footer can be cut; F6 → Keys lists load warnings only after a save; a dangling symlinked keys.toml becomes
   a plain file on save; with `insert_leader = ""` the ringing note shows no keys while typing; help rows wider than
   the key column shift their label; Copy is hidden for messages whose text is only in blocks; the README could say
   that a [global] binding now beats an equal pane default (Enter / Esc / Tab included, with a warning).

R. Asked by the user on 2026-10-19 (after v0.4.0): "how do i open the link? i've tried Ctrl+Shift+LMB" — klack had
   no way; offered a key, a menu entry and terminal-clickable links → "i want all three. also a picker when there's
   multiple things that can be opened in a message … multiple images for example aside from multiple links"; the
   plan played back → "yes, approved", plus: "a picker for `s` - save … pick one file to save or select multiple
   … configure a default path … choose another location":
  ~~R1~~ — done. `o` opens what a message holds: links (text, attachment / preview title links), pictures, videos, snippets;
      one → opened at once, several → a picker in message order; links in the browser (`xdg-open` / `open` /
      `start`, the address one argument, no shell; http, https, mailto only), pictures in the viewer at that one.
  ~~R2~~ — done (with the user's "context menu items should be contextual": open / save / copy only when they apply). The right-click / `m` menu gets "Open…" (same as `o`) on messages that hold something.
  ~~R3~~ — done. Links are OSC 8 hyperlinks in the message panes, so the terminal's own click (kitty: Ctrl+Shift+click)
      opens the address even when a label is shown or it wraps.
  ~~R4~~ — done. `s` opens a save dialog: the message's files ticked (Space one, `a` all/none), the folder below (Tab to
      edit, completing like `/upload`), Enter saves; never overwrites (" (1)"), creates the folder.
  ~~R5~~ — done. The default folder in F6 → Messages & media ("Save files to", settings.toml); Downloads when unset;
      `KLACK_DOWNLOAD_DIR` still wins.
  (Claude's calls, played back: picker order = message order; other files (PDF…) not in the open picker, `s`
  saves them; a one-file message also shows the save dialog.)

  ~~Q2~~ — done. BUG (the user, during R3): the reaction picker takes no mouse clicks and no wheel.
  ~~S~~ — done. (the user, during R3): Slack marks some messages, e.g. "Sent using @Claude" on messages sent through the
      Claude Code integration; klack shows no such marker. To find out where Slack keeps it.

L. Adjustments asked by the user on 2026-10-19 (after K, from using klack on their work workspace); one verified
   commit per step, in this order (the bug first):
  ~~L1~~ — done. BUG: a very long message (taller than the message pane) flickers: clicking it selects it, then the
      pane jumps back and forth between the message's start and end, and every mouse move forces another jump.
      Suspect the "keep the selection on screen" code in `MessagePane::draw` (it scrolls to the first line if the
      start is above the view, to the last if the end is below — a message taller than the view satisfies
      neither) fighting the hover redraw. Reproduce with a long message from B (more lines than the pane).
  ~~L2~~ — done. Threads opened from Ctrl+T (and probably any thread) do not scroll with the mouse wheel.
  ~~L3~~ — done. The Keys page of the preferences cannot be scrolled with the mouse wheel.
  ~~L4~~ — done. The preferences window stays small even when the terminal has room: let it grow with the terminal.
  ~~L5~~ — done. A context menu for the selected message from the keyboard (today only a right-click opens it): a key in
      the message list (and thread pane) opening the same menu at the message.
  ~~L6~~ — done. Narrow terminals: when there is no room for both the messages and a thread pane, opening a thread shows
      the thread in place of the messages (as Slack does); closing it brings the messages back. klack is hard
      to use at half a screen today; Slack stays usable. 
  ~~L7~~ — done. Found in L2: the composer's cursor shows through popups (a block in the Threads view at the composer's
      place); hide the cursor while a popup covers it.

J. Easier sign-in, asked by the user on 2026-10-19 ("streamline the login so it does not need copying the cookie
   via devtools"); they chose A as the main path with B and C as fallbacks; one verified commit per step:
  ~~J1~~ — done. A — `klack login` opens a throwaway Chrome/Chromium/Edge window (its own temporary profile) on
      Slack's sign-in page, the user signs in any way Slack offers (password, SSO, 2FA, magic link), klack takes
      the `d` cookie over the DevTools protocol and closes the window; the manual paste stays as the last resort.
  ~~J2~~ — done. B — `klack login --from-browser firefox|chrome`: import the `d` cookie from a browser already signed in
      (Firefox's cookies.sqlite; Chrome's encrypted store via the system keyring), read-only. Decided by the user
      2026-10-19: Firefox everywhere + Chrome/Chromium on Linux and macOS (Windows Chrome's app-bound encryption
      cannot be read by other programs); tested only on throwaway profiles seeded with B's cookie, never the
      user's real browser profiles; the user installs Firefox for the live test.
  ~~J3~~ — dropped by the user 2026-10-19 (Slack needs a reCAPTCHA for the code email; J1's window covers
      it). C — sign in by email code inside klack (Slack's internal email sign-in flow; not for SSO workspaces).
      The user reads the emailed code for the live test (ask which test account's inbox they can read).
      Investigated 2026-10-19 (the user signed B in by code in a recorded throwaway browser; flow in
      docs/protocol/auth.md): Slack sends the code only with a solved reCAPTCHA Enterprise
      (`signup.confirmEmail` without it → `captcha_empty`), so no terminal-only email sign-in is possible.
  Tests must not sign the test accounts out anywhere; temporary browser profiles are deleted afterwards.

## Known gaps / not verified

- Sign-in guide (W/Y): in one of five live runs the first list closed by itself ("Nothing signed in.") — most likely a lone ESC from a mouse-motion report split across reads (the picker enables any-motion tracking, ?1003) taken as Esc. Not reproduced since; a fix would drop motion tracking or ignore an Esc right after the list opens. The compact QR (80×24) fits addresses up to about 25 characters; longer ones get the link only there.
- Sign in "in your browser" (Y5) notices a new sign-in when the profile gains a new `d` cookie. Not yet checked with a real browser: that signing in to another workspace in a browser already signed in to Slack gives a new `d` (if it does not, the wait ends after 10 minutes with nothing found; the code way still works).
- A single long jump up (e.g. 56 000 lines at once) builds the whole distance once (~116 ms for 20 000 messages, release); paging and later frames are cheap.
- The unread line (H4g): with more unread than the first history page (50), the line's message is not loaded and no line shows; without `client.counts` (a failed request) there is no unread state at all.
- Drafts are saved 2 s after typing pauses (and on switches and leaving); a crash loses at most those last 2 s.
- The clipboard hand-off needs the terminal to outlive klack: with `kitty -e klack` the whole kitty instance exits and the copy is lost.
- Video: seeking ±10 s (I13; its audio side not heard live); audio/video sync is by wall clock (both start together, no drift correction); each frame is a new kitty image (kitty evicts old ones by its storage quota); needs `ffmpeg`/`ffprobe` on PATH (a clear error otherwise).
- Microphone: the capture pipeline (`MicConverter`: mixdown, resampling, 20 ms framing) is unit-tested with a synthetic 44.1 kHz stereo tone; opening the device is not verified live. A live attempt with `PIPEWIRE_NODE` pointing at a temporary null sink did **not** route the capture there — the default microphone (the user's headset) was used for ~12 s into the private test huddle; the recording was deleted. Do not run microphone tests without explicit device selection.
- Huddle view: layouts with many people are verified by unit tests only — one Slack user gets the same attendee id on every join (a second session of B kicks the first; a third share got signaling error 206), so live tests top out at 2 people + their camera and share.
- Right after a huddle empties, Slack can still hand out its room (same room and attendee id) though the meeting is over; the session then ends before connecting (seen twice in ~15 tries, F3e). The TUI joins again once after 2 s; `ctl huddle-media` (a dev tool) does not. A second F3 while "joining the huddle…" sends another `rooms.join` (its answer is ignored once the call exists).
- Built and tested from a clean checkout on Debian 13, Ubuntu 24.04, Fedora and Arch (containers); not on other distributions, BSDs or musl. Since H5, CI builds and tests on Ubuntu 24.04 and macOS 14 (arm) and cross-builds Windows on every push; the v0.1.0 downloads were run on Linux, macOS 26 (arm) and Windows 11 — the Intel macOS build was not run (no Rosetta on the test Mac), and the Windows tests never run (cross-built).
- Huddles: roster departures follow the SDK rule (an AUDIO_STREAM_ID_INFO entry with neither attendee id nor muted = left), found when B kept A after A had left; microphone processing (echo cancellation, noise suppression, gain, speaking gate) is unit-tested with synthetic signals only — echo cancellation is verified live only through a virtual loopback (a clean digital echo; no acoustic test — the user has no free-standing mic or speaker); no video or screen share.
- Commands with interactive answers (buttons in an ephemeral message) show the buttons as text only.
- Thumbnails: tested in kitty, xterm (sixel as a VT340, half blocks), foot (sixel) and WezTerm (iTerm2; a WezTerm over ssh, without `TERM_PROGRAM`, would still get the kitty protocol it cannot draw — `KLACK_GRAPHICS=iterm2` then); Konsole and other terminals that answer the kitty query without kitty's placeholders are not tried; a failed thumbnail says why in its reserved rows (F3b); GIFs show their first frame only.
- Notifications: a click opens the conversation only on XDG desktops (Linux/BSD), only within 30 minutes, and does not raise the terminal window; the quiet hours follow DST in the zone klack shows times in (I8), which may differ from the zone set on Slack.
- A freshly opened kitty window does not report focus-out until it has had one focus-in, so right after launch an unfocused klack may think it is focused (harness quirk; normal use is unaffected once the window has been focused).
- Workspaces: the others are polled for unread counts every 30 s (status bar) and watched for notifications, but their timelines are not kept; switching reloads everything (no per-workspace state is kept).
- Profile pictures: bots and apps get the icon their message carries (I4; a real app picture not seen live — no app in the test workspace); pictures are cached for the session only (a changed picture is loaded afresh since I5, NOT seen live); drawn with kitty, sixel (foot) and iTerm2 (WezTerm).
- Sections: built-in sections other than Starred/Channels/Direct messages (Slack Connect, Recent Apps, Agents, …) are not shown; no per-section sort (folding: H4i; emoji: I11).
- Snippets: Slack may relabel a tiny snippet by its content (`fn main() {}` became C++; klack still highlights by extension); snippets are not editable; long snippet scrolling LIVE since A5 (from the thread pane).
- Channels: a previewed channel is fetched again every 15 s (configurable, I10), not live (Slack sends no RTM events for channels you are not in); the Ctrl+K list keeps up to 2 000 entries (I9); no archive, rename, invite or topic editing; the public channel list is loaded once after boot (new channels arrive over RTM).

## Bug log

- The status bar's "N unread" counted conversations the sidebar does not show as unread — muted ones (dimmed, as in Slack), archived channels, DMs with deactivated people — so it could say "4 unread" with nothing to find. Reported by the user from their work workspace (P1); reproduced with a muted test channel; the count now uses the sidebar's own rule (`the_unread_count_is_what_the_sidebar_shows`).

- The composer's cursor was cells away from the text once a word wrapped: the text was drawn word-wrapped by ratatui while the cursor was counted as if rows broke at any character. Reported by the user from real use (M1); the composer now wraps the text itself and the drawing and the cursor share those rows (`the_cursor_follows_wrapped_words`).

- A message taller than the message pane flickered between its start and its end when selected: the selection was kept on screen at every draw, by its last line when its end was below the view and by its first when its start was above — a message taller than the view always had one of the two, so each redraw (each pointer move) flipped it. Found by the user on their work workspace (L1). The selection is now brought into view once, when it changes, a tall one from its start (regression test `a_message_taller_than_the_view_stays_put_when_selected`).

- Attachments without a title showed their `fallback` as a bold title above their text — `fallback` is a plain-text copy, so the text came twice, the first time raw (backticks, `<url|label>`). Found in I6 with Slackbot's answer to `/remind help`; `fallback` now shows only when there is no title and no text (regression test `attachments_show_their_fallback_only_when_there_is_nothing_else`).

- A picture first drawn while a dialog covered it never showed (an empty box until restart): ratatui-image sends a picture to kitty once, in front of its first placeholder cell, and marks it sent; the dialog's `Clear` (and its text) overwrote that cell in the same frame. Found in H4h by turning two-row pictures on in F6 (the new pictures load under the dialog). `clear_popup` now holds that data and the frame puts it back in front of whatever covers the cell (a virtual placement shows nothing there), with the cell's own width forced for ratatui's diff (regression test `pictures_first_drawn_under_a_popup_still_reach_the_terminal`).

- A window squeezed to a few cells crashed klack ("min > max" in `f64::clamp`): image thumbnails were sized with a maximum of 0 columns. Found in H3 by shrinking the test window step by step with popups open; a pane with no room now gets no thumbnail (regression test `no_room_no_thumbnail`).

- Up on the oldest loaded message never loaded older history when the lines above it (its day separator) were off screen: `maybe_load_older` needed the very top line visible, and keeping the selection in view stops at its own lines. Found in the F3f regression test (the previous binary did the same); Up there now loads older messages directly (`load_history` guards against double loads and the end of history).

- An edit started in one conversation and finished after switching to another was looked up in the new conversation and silently lost (one composer for all conversations; `save_edit` uses the current one). Found by reading the code while adding drafts (not seen live); drafts now keep the edit with its conversation. The single composer also carried typed text into the next conversation.

- Muted channels were never recognised: boot's `prefs.muted_channels` is null on current workspaces; mutes live in `all_notifications_prefs.channels[*].muted`. Found while reverse engineering the notification prefs; now read from there (and live from `pref_change`). Test `notification_prefs_from_slack`.

- B's sidebar listed a DM as "USLACK": Slack's system user (`USLACK`, "Slack", trial notices) is not returned by `users.list`. Seen while testing workspace switching; DM partners missing from the user list are now looked up with `users.info` after boot. Test `dm_partners_missing_from_the_user_list`; live, B's sidebar shows "Slack".

- Scheduled quiet hours were assumed to repeat every day, so with a weekday-only schedule (17:00–09:00) the app thought DND was on on a Sunday evening and would have kept notifications quiet; seen as a stray 🔕 in the new status bar. `dnd.info` then reports the next window (Mon 17:00 → Tue 09:00). Now only the window ending at `next_dnd_end_ts` counts (its length being the schedule's daily span), and `dnd.info` is asked again once it has passed. The `dnd_windows` test has the real values.

- Day separators and the thread "N replies" rule were attributed to the next message, so selecting it highlighted the rule too. Found by screenshot after a search jump; tests now assert they belong to no message.

- The `WideSafe` filter (previous fix) measured the raw cell symbol, so a kitty image cell carrying the image transmission escape (thousands of characters, forced width 1) looked thousands of columns wide and the rest of its row was dropped: the viewer image lost its top row. Found by screenshot; now uses `Cell::cell_width()`, which honours forced widths. Test `forced_width_cells_cover_nothing`.

- VS16 emoji (❤️) shifted the rest of their row by one column (`[❤  1]`) and left stale characters after scrolling or switching conversation. Reproduced in isolation with a two-frame ratatui program in kitty. Cause: ratatui-core 0.1.2 re-emits the emoji's trailing cell right after it; the crossterm backend sees an adjacent cell and skips the cursor move, but the terminal had already advanced two columns. Fixed with a backend wrapper (`terminal::WideSafe`) that drops writes into columns covered by the wide character just written. Test `covered_columns_are_dropped`; the same live scenario is clean now.

- Notifications never fired: `dnd.info` reports the *next start* and *next end* separately (today 00:00 and tomorrow 09:00 for a daily 00:00–09:00 schedule), and reading them as one span made DND look active for 33 h. Found with a debug log of each notification decision; now read as a daily schedule. Test `dnd_windows` uses the real values.

- `message_replied` carries the parent *without* reactions/files, and replacing the parent wiped its reactions (👍 vanished after any reply). Confirmed by capturing A's RTM events with `ctl listen`; now only the thread fields are merged. `message_changed` likewise keeps reactions, reply counts and files when the event omits them (a missing `reply_count` reset it to 0, caught by the new test). Test `message_replied_keeps_reactions`.

- Live thread replies were counted twice (`💬 3 replies` for 2): the reply event bumped `reply_count` and `message_replied` also carried the server count. Found by screenshot; a reply is now counted only if newer than the parent's `latest_reply`. Test `reply_counted_once_with_message_replied`.
- " (edited)" was appended after wrapping and got cut off in narrow panes. Found by screenshot in the thread pane; now wraps to its own line. Test `edited_mark_wraps_instead_of_overflowing`.

- Duplicate day separator after system messages (`channel_join`). Found by screenshot; fixed in 962ca69 and guarded by the test `one_day_separator_around_system_messages`.
- Status-bar unread counts were computed only at boot. Found by screenshot; now computed per frame (3acf67d).
